\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}x^{-1}{W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)}=2\xi.

(4.19)

Proof.

We prove Item
(i)
last.

Item
(ii)
follows from the same property in rational directions (Theorem
4.3.3
(ii)
).

Item
(iii)
:
The monotonicity of the horizontal Busemann process from Theorem
4.3.3
(v)
extends to all directions by limits. That is, for any two rational directions
\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}
and any real
x<y
, and
t
,

W_{\xi_{1}-}(y,t;x,t)\leq W_{\xi_{1}}(y,t;x,t)\leq W_{\xi_{1}+}(y,t;x,t)\leq W_{\xi_{2}-}(y,t;x,t),

(4.20)

and when
\xi_{1}\notin\mathbb{Q}
, the same monotonicity holds, removing the middle term that does not distinguish between
\pm
.
Hence, the limits as
\alpha\nearrow\xi
and
\alpha\searrow\xi
exist and agree with the limits from rational directions (without the

\textstyle\boxempty

). Without loss of generality, we take the compact set to be
[a,b]^{2}
. Then, by (
4.20
) and Lemma
A.1.2
, for
\alpha<\xi
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
a\leq x\leq y\leq b
,

0\leq W_{\xi-}(y,t;x,t)-W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;x,t)\leq W_{\xi-}(b,t;a,t)-W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(b,t;a,t),

(4.21)

and for general
(x,y)\in[a,b]^{2}
,

|W_{\xi-}(y,t;x,t)-W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;x,t)|\leq|W_{\xi-}(b,t;a,t)-W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(b,t;a,t)|,

so the limit as
\alpha\nearrow\xi
is uniform on compacts. An analogous argument applies to
\alpha\searrow\xi
.

Item
(iv)
:

For
t,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, the continuity of

(x,y)\mapsto W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;x,t)

follows from Item
(iii)
and the continuity for rational
\xi
in Theorem
4.3.3
(iv)
. Before showing the general continuity, we show the limits (
4.19
). For
\xi,t\in\mathbb{Q}
, (
4.13
) holds by definition of
\Omega_{1}
.
Keeping
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
, let
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
t>s
be rational. By Theorem
4.3.3
(ii)
–
(iii)
,

\displaystyle W_{\xi}(x,s;0,s)

\displaystyle=W_{\xi}(x,s;0,t)+W_{\xi}(0,t;0,s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi}(z,t;0,t)\}+W_{\xi}(0,t;0,s).

Then, by Lemma
3.5.4
(for the temporally reflected
\mathcal{L}
),

\displaystyle\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}x^{-1}{W_{\xi}(x,s;0,s)}=2\xi.

Now, let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
t\in\mathbb{R}
be arbitrary. Then, the monotonicity of (
4.20
) implies that for
\alpha<\xi<\beta
with
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
,

\alpha\leq\liminf_{x\to\infty}x^{-1}{W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)}\leq\limsup_{x\to\infty}x^{-1}{W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)}\leq\beta.

Sending
\mathbb{Q}\ni\alpha\nearrow\xi
and
\mathbb{Q}\ni\beta\searrow\xi
implies (
4.19
) for
+\infty
. The case
x\to-\infty
follows a symmetric argument.

Lastly, the continuity of
(x,y)\mapsto W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;x,t)
and (
4.19
) imply that
W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)\leq a+b|x|
for some constants
a,b
. The general continuity follows from (
4.17
) and Lemma
3.5.3
(i)
.

Item
(i)
:

The statement holds for all
x,y,t,\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
by Item
(v)
of
\Omega_{1}
. The continuity proved in Item
(iv)
extends this to all
x,y,t\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

Recall Definition
4.4.7
of the extreme maximizers
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L/R}(t)
.

Lemma 4.4.13
.

For each
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
,
(x,s;y,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\textstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\uparrow$}}}}^{4}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,

\lim_{z\to\pm\infty}\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;y,t)=-\infty

(4.22)

so that
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L/R}
are well-defined.
Let
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be a compact set,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
. Then, there exists a random
Z=Z(\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},K)\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
x,s,t\in K
with
s<t
and
S\in\{L,R\}
,
|g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)|\leq Z
.

Proof.

By the continuity and asymptotics of Lemma
4.4.12
(iv)
, for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists
a,b>0
such that
|W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)|\leq a+b|x|
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Lemma
B.1.2
implies
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)\sim-\frac{(z-x)^{2}}{t-s}
, which gives (
4.22
).
Next we observe that

\displaystyle\inf_{x,s,t\in K,s<t}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)\}

(4.23)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{x,s,t\in K,s<t}\mathcal{L}(x,s;x,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)>-\infty.

The last inequality is justified as follows. Since
W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)
evolves backwards in time as the KPZ fixed point (
4.17
), Lemma
3.5.3
(ii)
implies that
a
and
b
can be chosen uniformly for
t\in K
. Lemma
B.1.2
states that
\forall x,s,t\in\mathbb{R}
with
s<t
, there is a constant
C
such that

\mathcal{L}(x,s;x,t)\geq-C(t-s)^{1/3}\log^{2}\bigl(\tfrac{2\sqrt{2x^{2}+s^{2}+t^{2}}+4}{(t-s)\wedge 1}\bigr).

Taking the infimum over
x,s,t\in K
with
s<t
yields the last inequality in (
4.23
).

To contradict the last statement of the lemma, assume there exist maximizers
z_{n}
of
\mathcal{L}(x_{n},s_{n};z,t_{n})+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t_{n};0,t_{n})
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
such that
x_{n},s_{n},t_{n}\in K
but
|z_{n}|\to\infty
.
Then, by (
4.23
),

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{L}(x_{n},s_{n};z_{n},t_{n})+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z_{n},t_{n};0,t_{n})>-\infty,

(4.24)

but since
z_{n}\to\infty
and
x_{n},s_{n},t_{n}\in K
for all
n
,
\mathcal{L}(x_{n},s_{n};z_{n};t_{n})\sim-\frac{(z_{n}-x_{n})^{2}}{t_{n}-s_{n}}
by Lemma
B.1.2
. By the bound
|W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)|\leq a+b|x|
that holds uniformly for
t\in K
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, the inequality (
4.24
) cannot hold.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4.1
, Items
(i)
–
(iv)
.

The full-probability event of these items is
\Omega_{1}
. The remaining items are proved later.

Item
(i)
(Continuity):
This was proved in Lemma
4.4.12
(iv)
.

Item
(ii)
(Additivity):
First, we show that on
\Omega_{1}
for
s<t
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,

-\infty<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}-,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}+,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}-,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}+,S}(t)<\infty.

(4.25)

The finiteness of the maximizers comes from Lemma
4.4.13
. The rest of (
4.25
) follows from the monotonicity of (
4.20
) and Lemma
A.1.1
. Next, we show that for
(x,s;y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
W_{\alpha}(x,s;y,t)
converges pointwise to
W_{\xi-}(x,s;y,t)
as
\mathbb{Q}\ni\alpha\nearrow\xi
. The same holds for limits from the right, with
\xi-
replaced by
\xi+
(Later we prove that the convergence is locally uniform). By (
4.17
), it suffices to assume
s<t
. By (
4.25
) and the additivity of Lemma
4.4.12
(ii)
when
s=t
, for all
\alpha\in[\xi-1,\xi+1]\cap\mathbb{Q}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,

\displaystyle W_{\alpha}(x,s;y,t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\alpha}(z,t;y,t)\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\alpha}(z,t;0,t)\}+W_{\alpha}(0,t;y,t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in[g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi-1)-,L}(t),g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi+1)+,R}(t)]}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\alpha}(z,t;0,t)\}+W_{\alpha}(0,t;y,t).

By Lemma
4.4.12
(iii)
,
W_{\alpha}(z,t;y,t)
converges uniformly on compact sets to
W_{\xi-}(x,t;y,t)
as
\mathbb{Q}\ni\alpha\nearrow\xi
and to
W_{\xi+}(x,t;y,t)
as
\mathbb{Q}\ni\alpha\searrow\xi
. This implies the desired pointwise convergence. The additivity follows from the additivity for rational
\xi
(Theorem
4.3.3
(ii)
).

Item
(iii)
(Monotonicity along a horizontal line):
This was previously proven as Equation (
4.20
).

Item
(iv)
(Backwards evolution as the KPZ fixed point):
This follows directly from the construction (
4.17
).

Item
(v)
is proved after the proof of Theorem
4.4.3
, and Items
(vii)
–
(viii)
are proved after the proof of Theorem
4.6.3
. No subsequent results depend on Items
(vii)
–
(viii)
, except the mixing in Theorem
4.4.3
(ii)
, proven later.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4.3
(Distributional properties).

Item
(i)
(Independence):
We

know that, for
T\in\mathbb{R}
,
\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t):s,y\in\mathbb{R},s<t\leq T\}
is independent of
\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t):s,y\in\mathbb{R},T\leq s<t\}
. From the definition of the Busemann process and (
4.16
)–(
4.17
), the process

\{W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t):\xi\in\mathbb{R},\,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\},\,x,y\in\mathbb{R},\,s,t\geq T\}

is a function of

\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t):s,y\in\mathbb{R},T\leq s<t\}
, and independence follows.

Item
(ii)
(Stationarity):

Similarly as the previous item, the stationarity of the process follows from the stationarity of the directed landscape from Lemma
B.1.1
(i)
. The mixing properties will be proven in Section
4.7.1
, along with Items
(vii)
–
(viii)
of Theorem
4.4.1
.

Item
(iii)
(Distribution along a time level):
By the additivity of Theorem
4.4.1
(ii)
and the variational definition (
4.17
), for
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
s<t
, and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, on the full-probability event
\Omega_{1}
,

\displaystyle W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;0,s)=W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;0,t)-W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(0,s;0,t)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)\}-\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(0,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)\}.

By Item
(i)
, Theorem
4.4.1
(iii)
, and Items
(iii)
and
(iv)
of Lemma
4.4.12
,
\{W_{\xi+}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},t;0,t):\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
is a reverse-time Markov process that almost surely lies in the state space
\mathcal{Y}
defined in (
3.43
). By the stationarity of Item
(ii)
, the law of
\{W_{\xi+}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},t;0,t):\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}
must be invariant for this process. By the temporal reflection invariance of the directed landscape (Lemma
B.1.1
(iii)
),
\{W_{\xi+}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},t;0,t):\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}
is also invariant for the KPZ fixed point, forward in time. The uniqueness part of Theorem
3.2.2
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.4.14
.

For every
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
and
(x,s;y,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\textstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\uparrow$}}}}^{4}
,
\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)\leq W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t)
, and equality occurs if and only if
y
maximizes
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

For
s<t
, Theorem
4.4.1
(ii)
,
(iv)
gives

\displaystyle W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;y,t)\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)\}+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(0,t;y,t).

(4.26)

Setting
z=y
on the right-hand side of (
4.4.3
), it follows that
W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t)\geq\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)
, and equality holds if and only if
y
is a maximizer.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4.9
(Construction of the Busemann geodesics).

The

full-probability event of this theorem is
\Omega_{1}
(
4.12
).

Item
(i)
(Finiteness of the maximizers):
This follows immediately from Lemma
4.4.13
.

We prove
Items
(ii)
–
(iv)
together. By Lemma
4.4.14
, for any such construction of a path from the sequence of times
s=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots
and any
i\geq 1
,

\mathcal{L}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t_{i}),t_{i})=W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t_{i}),t_{i}).

Furthermore, for any
t_{i-1}\leq t<u\leq t_{i}
, it must hold that

\mathcal{L}(g(t),t;g(u),u)=W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t),t;g(u),u),

for otherwise, by additivity of the Busemann functions (Theorem
4.4.1
(ii)
),

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{L}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t_{i}),t_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t),t)+\mathcal{L}(g(t),t;g(u),u)+\mathcal{L}(g(u),u;g(t_{i}),t_{i})

\displaystyle<W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t),t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t),t;g(u),u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(u),u;g(t_{i}),t_{i})

\displaystyle=W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t_{i-1}),t_{i-1};g(t_{i}),t_{i}),

a contradiction. Additivity extends (
4.11
) to all
s\leq t<u
. Therefore, the path is a semi-infinite geodesic because the weight of the path in between any two points is optimal by Lemma
4.4.14
. From the equality (
4.11
) and Lemma
4.4.14
, for
every

t\geq s
,
g(t)
maximizes
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
.

Figure 4.2
:
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
4.4.15
. Here, the red/thick path denotes the path
\hat{\gamma}
in the case
w_{t}<g(t)
, which is to the right of the rightmost geodesic between
(x,s)
and
(g(u),u)
, which passes through
(w_{t},t)
by assumption. This gives the contradiction.

Before global directedness of all geodesics, we show that
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}
are semi-infinite geodesics and the leftmost/rightmost geodesics between any two of their points. Take
S=R
, and the result for
S=L
follows similarly. Omit
x,s,\xi
, and

\textstyle\boxempty

from the notation temporarily, and write
g(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)
.
By what was just proved, it is sufficient to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4.15
.

Let
g
be as defined above. For
s<t<u
, let
z_{u}
be the rightmost maximizer of
\mathcal{L}(g(t),t;z,u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,u;0,u)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
w_{t}
be the rightmost maximizer of
\mathcal{L}(x,s;w,t)+\mathcal{L}(w,t;g(u),u)
over
w\in\mathbb{R}

(
Equivalently,
(w_{t},t)
is the point at level
t
on the rightmost geodesic between
(x,s)
and
(g(u),u)
)
. Then,
g(t)=w_{t}
and
g(u)=z_{u}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4.14
and Items
(ii)
(ii)(b)
–
(ii)(c)
,
w_{t}
maximizes
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
, and
z_{u}
maximizes
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,u;0,u)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
. By definition of
g(u)
and
g(t)
as the rightmost maximizers, we have
w_{t}\leq g(t)
and
z_{u}\leq g(u)
in general. Assume, to the contrary, that
g(t)\neq w_{t}
or
g(u)\neq z_{u}
. We first prove a contradiction in the case
w_{t}<g(t)
. For the proof, refer to Figure
4.2
for clarity. Let
\gamma_{1}:[s,u]\to\mathbb{R}
be the rightmost geodesic from
(x,s)
to
(g(u),u)
(which passes through
(w_{t},t)
), and let
\gamma_{2}
be the concatenation of the rightmost geodesic from
(x,s)
to
(g(t),t)
followed by the rightmost geodesic from
(g(t),t)
to
(z_{u},u)
. By Item
(ii)
(ii)(b)
for
i=1,2
, the weight of the portion of any part of
\gamma_{i}
is equal to the Busemann function between the points. Since
w_{t}<g(t)
and
z_{u}\leq g(u)
,
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
must split before time
t
, and then meet again before or at time
u
. Let
(y,v)
be a crossing point, where
t<v\leq u
. Let
\hat{\gamma}:[s,u]\to\mathbb{R}
be defined by
\hat{\gamma}(r)=\gamma_{2}(r)
for
r\in[s,v]
and
\hat{\gamma}(r)=\gamma_{1}(r)
from
(y,v)
to
(g(u),u)
. Then, by the additivity of Busemann functions, the weight
\mathcal{L}
of any portion of the path
\hat{\gamma}
is equal to the Busemann function between the two points. By Lemma
4.4.14
,
\widehat{\gamma}
is then a geodesic between
(x,s)
and
(g(u),u)
, which is to the right of
\gamma_{1}
, which was defined to be the rightmost geodesic between the points, a contradiction.

Now, we consider the case
z_{u}<g(u)
. Define
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
as in the previous case. Since
z_{u}<g(u)
, there is some point
(y,v)
with
t\leq v<u
such that
\gamma_{1}
splits from or crosses
\gamma_{2}
at
(y,v)
. Then, define
\hat{\gamma}
as in the previous case. Again, the weight
\mathcal{L}
of any portion of the path
\hat{\gamma}
is equal to the Busemann function between the two points. Specifically,
\mathcal{L}(g(t),t;g(u),u)=W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(g(t),t;g(u),u)
, and by Item
4.4.14
,
g(u)
maximizes
\mathcal{L}(g(t),t;z,u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,u;0,u)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
. This contradicts the definition of
z_{u}
as the rightmost such maximizer.
∎

Returning to the proof of Theorem
4.4.9
, we show the global directedness of all Busemann geodesics constructed in the manner described in Item
(ii)
. By (
4.25
), for
t\geq s
and
\alpha<\xi<\beta
with
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha,L}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)\leq g(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\beta,R}(t).

(4.27)

Note that on
\Omega_{1}
the
\pm
distinction is absent for
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
(Lemma
4.4.12
(i)
).
By definition (
4.12
) of the event
\Omega_{1}
and Theorem
4.3.3
(iii)
,
\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
, the maximizers of
\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\alpha}(z,t;0,t)
over
z\in\mathbb{R}
are exactly the locations
z
where an
\alpha
-directed geodesic goes through
(z,t)
. Therefore,
g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha,L}(t)/t\to\alpha
and
g_{(x,s)}^{\beta,R}(t)/t\to\beta
when
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
. By (
4.27
),

\alpha\leq\liminf_{t\to\infty}t^{-1}{g(t)}\leq\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^{-1}{g(t)}\leq\beta.

Sending
\mathbb{Q}\ni\alpha\nearrow\xi
and
\mathbb{Q}\ni\beta\searrow\xi
completes the proof of Theorem
4.4.9
.
∎

We now define the next full-probability event.

Let
\Omega_{2}
be the subset of
\Omega_{1}
on which the following hold.

(4.28)

(i)

For each integer
T\in\mathbb{Z}
and each compact set
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
, there exists
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\xi,T,K)>0
such that for
\xi-\varepsilon<\alpha<\xi<\beta<\xi+\varepsilon
and
(x,y)\in K
,

W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,T;x,T)=W_{\xi-}(y,T;x,T)\ \ \text{ and }\ \ W_{\beta{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,T;x,T)=W_{\xi+}(y,T;x,T).

(4.29)

(ii)

For each integer
T\in\mathbb{Z}
, the set

\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:W_{\xi-}(x,T;0,T)\neq W_{\xi+}(x,T;0,T)\text{ for some }x\in\mathbb{R}\}

(4.30)

is countably infinite and dense in
\mathbb{R}
.

(iii)

For each
s<t\in\mathbb{R}
,
x,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,

\lim_{\xi\to\pm\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=\pm\infty.

(4.31)

Lemma 4.4.16
.

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2})=1.

Proof.

Recall the distributional equality

\{W_{\xi+}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},T;0,T)\}_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\overset{d}{=}\{G^{\sqrt{2}}_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}
from Theorem
4.4.3
(iii)
.
The fact that
(i)
holds with probability one is a direct consequence of the regularity of the SH from
2.5.2
(iii)
. The set (
4.30
) is countably infinite and dense for all
T\in\mathbb{Z}
by the corresponding properties of
G^{\sqrt{2}}
from Theorem
2.5.2
(iv)
,
(vi)
.

Now, we prove that (
4.31
) holds with probability one. By the monotonicity of (
4.25
), the limits
\lim_{\xi\to\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)
and
\lim_{\xi\to-\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)
exist in
\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
. Furthermore, by this monotonicity, it is sufficient to show that

\lim_{\xi\to\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)=\infty\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \lim_{\xi\to-\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t)=\inf_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t)=-\infty.

(4.32)

First, we show that (
4.32
) holds with probability one for a fixed initial point
(x,s)
and fixed
t>s
. It is therefore sufficient to take
(x,s)=(0,0)
and then
t>0
. By the monotonicity, it suffices to take limits over
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
so that by Theorem
4.3.1
(iii)
, the
\pm
and
L/R
distinctions are unnecessary.
W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,t;0,t)
is a two-sided Brownian motion with drift
2\xi
and diffusivity
\sqrt{2}
, independent of the random function
(x,y)\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x,0;y,t)
(Theorem
4.4.3
(i)
). Let
B
be a standard Brownian motion, independent of
\mathcal{L}
. Using skew stationarity with
c=-\xi
in the third equality below and time stationarity in the fifth equality (Lemma
B.1.1
), we obtain, for
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
,

\displaystyle g_{(x,s)}^{\xi}(t)

\displaystyle=\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi}(z,t;0,t)\}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+\sqrt{2}B(z)+2\xi z\}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x-\xi s,s;z-\xi t,t)+2\xi(x-z)+(t-s)\xi^{2}+\sqrt{2}B(z)+2\xi z\}

\displaystyle=\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x-\xi s,s;z-\xi t,t)+\sqrt{2}(B(z)-B(\xi(t-s)))\}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z-\xi(t-s),t)+\sqrt{2}B(z-\xi(t-s))\}

\displaystyle=\argmax_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+\sqrt{2}B(z)\}+\xi(t-s)\overset{d}{=}g_{(x,s)}^{0}(t)+\xi(t-s).

Therefore,
\forall\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
, the distribution of
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi}(t)
is that of a fixed, almost surely finite, random variable plus
\xi(t-s)
. Since we know
\lim_{\mathbb{Q}\ni\xi\to\pm\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi}(t)
exists, the limit must be
\pm\infty
a.s.

Now, consider the intersection of
\Omega_{1}
with event of probability one on which for each triple
(w,q_{1},q_{2})\in\mathbb{Q}^{3}
with
q_{1}<q_{2}
,

\lim_{\xi\to+\infty}g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi-,L}(q_{2})=+\infty\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{\xi\to-\infty}g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi+,R}(q_{2})=-\infty.

(4.33)

On this event, let
(x,s,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
s<t
be arbitrary. Assume, by way of contradiction, that

z:=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)<\infty,

(4.34)

and let
g:[s,t]
denote the leftmost geodesic from
(x,s)
to
(z,t)
. For this proof, refer to Figure
4.3
for clarity. By the assumption (
4.34
) and the fact that
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}
is the leftmost geodesic between any two of its points (Theorem
4.4.9
(iv)
),
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)\leq g(t)
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
t>s
.
Let
q_{1}\in(s,t)
be rational. Choose
w\in\mathbb{Q}
such that
w<g(q_{1})
. By continuity of geodesics, we may choose
q_{2}\in(q_{1},t)\cap\mathbb{Q}
to be sufficiently close to
t
so that
|g(q_{2})-z|<1
. Next, by (
4.33
), we may choose positive
\xi
sufficiently large so that

g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi-,L}(q_{2})>z+1>g(q_{2})\geq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(q_{2}).

(4.35)

Since
w<g(q_{1})
,
g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi-,L}
and
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}
cross at some
(\hat{z},\hat{t})
with
\hat{t}\in(q_{1},q_{2})
. By Theorem
4.4.9
(iii)
, both
g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi-,L}(q_{2})
and
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(q_{2})
are the leftmost maximizer of
\mathcal{L}(\hat{z},\hat{t};y,q_{2})+W_{\xi-}(y,q_{2};0,q_{2})
over
y\in\mathbb{R}
, contradicting (
4.35
). The proof for
\xi\to-\infty
is analogous.
∎

Figure 4.3
:
The blue/thin path represents
g_{(w,q_{1})}^{\xi-,L}
and the red/thick path represents
g
.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.1
(v)
(Regularity of the Busemann process).

By definition of the

event
\Omega_{2}
(
4.28
), for each
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, each integer
T
and compact set
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
, there is a
\varepsilon>0
so that (
4.29
) holds for all
(x,y)\in K
. Now, let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, let
K
be a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and let
T
be an integer greater than
\sup\{t\vee s:(x,s;y,t)\in K\}
. Let

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\inf\{g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi-1)-,L}(T)\wedge g_{(y,t)}^{(\xi-1)-,L}(T):(x,s;y,t)\in K\},\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\sup\{g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi+1)+,R}(T)\vee g_{(y,t)}^{(\xi+1)+,R}(T):(x,s;y,t)\in K\}.

By (
4.25
) and Lemma
4.4.13
,
-\infty<A<B<\infty
.
By additivity (Theorem
4.4.1
(ii)
) and (
4.25
), for all
(x,s;y,t)\in K
and
\alpha\in(\xi-1,\xi+1)
,

\displaystyle W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t)=W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;0,T)-W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,T)

(4.36)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,T)+W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}-\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(y,t;z,T)+W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in[A,B]}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,T)+W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}-\sup_{z\in[A,B]}\{\mathcal{L}(y,t;z,T)+W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}.

By (
4.29
), the conclusion follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4.5
(Description of the discontinuity set).

The full

probability event of this theorem is
\Omega_{2}
, except for Item
(ii)
whose proof is postponed until Section
4.7.2
. The only result that relies on this Item is Theorem
4.2.6
, which is proved afterward.

Item
(i)
(Monotonicity):

By the monotonicity of Theorem
4.4.1
(v)
, and by Lemma
A.1.2
, for
a\leq x\leq y\leq b
,

0\leq W_{\xi+}(y,t;x,t)-W_{\xi-}(y,t;x,t)\leq W_{\xi+}(b,t;a,t)-W_{\xi-}(b,t;a,t).

(4.37)

Thus, discontinuities of
\xi\mapsto W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;x,t)
are also discontinuities for
\xi\mapsto W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(b,t;a,t)
.

Item
(iii)
(
\Xi
is a countable dense set):

Similarly as in (
4.36
), if
(x,s;y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
, then for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and any integer
T>s\vee t
,

\displaystyle W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,s;y,t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,T)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}

(4.38)

\displaystyle-\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\{\mathcal{L}(y,t;z,T)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)\}.

So if
W_{\xi-}(z,T;0,T)=W_{\xi+}(z,T;0,T)\;\forall z\in\mathbb{R}
, then
W_{\xi-}(x,s;y,t)=W_{\xi+}(x,s;y,t)
, and

\Xi=\bigcup_{T\in\mathbb{Z}}\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}:W_{\xi-}(x,T;0,T)\neq W_{\xi+}(x,T;0,T)\text{ for some }x\in\mathbb{R}\}.

(4.39)

On
\Omega_{2}
,
\Xi
is countably infinite and dense by (
4.28
). Lemma
4.4.12
(i)
, along with (
4.39
) imply that
\Xi
contains no rational directions
\xi
. For an arbitrary
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
W_{\xi-}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},T;0,T)
and
W_{\xi+}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\bullet$}}},T;0,T)
are both Brownian motions with the same diffusivity and drift, and
W_{\xi-}(y,T;x,T)\leq W_{\xi+}(y,T;x,T)
for
x<y
by Theorem
4.4.1
(iii)
. By (
4.39
) and continuity,

\mathbb{P}(\xi\in\Xi)\leq\sum_{T\in\mathbb{Z},x\in\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{P}(W_{\xi-}(x,T;0,T)\neq W_{\xi+}(x,T;0,T))=0,

where
\mathbb{P}(W_{\xi-}(x,T;0,T)\neq W_{\xi+}(x,T;0,T))=0
because the two random variables have the same law and are ordered.

Item
(iv)
(
\Xi(p;q)
is discrete):
The discreteness of the jumps is a direct consequence of the regularity of
the Busemann process from Theorem
4.4.1
(v)
. The fact that
\Xi(y,t;x,t)
is countably infinite and unbounded on a
t
-dependent full probability event follows from Theorem
2.5.2
(iv)

∎

4.5
Non-uniqueness of semi-infinite geodesics

Theorem
4.4.9
established global existence of semi-infinite geodesics from each initial point and into each direction.
We know from Theorem 3.3 of
[
126
]
, recorded earlier in Theorem
4.3.1
(iii)
, that for a fixed initial point and a fixed direction, there almost surely is a unique semi-infinite geodesic. However, this uniqueness does not extend globally to all initial points and directions simultaneously.

In fact, two qualitatively different types of non-uniqueness of Busemann geodesics from a given point into a given direction arise. One is denoted by the
L/R
distinction and the other by the
\pm
distinction.
All semi-infinite geodesics from
p
in direction
\xi
lie between the leftmost Busemann geodesic
g_{p}^{\xi-,L}
and the rightmost Busemann geodesic
g_{p}^{\xi+,R}
. See Theorem
4.5.7
(i)
. We refer the reader back to Figure
4.1
for the two types of non-uniqueness. The
L/R
uniqueness is depicted on the left, where geodesics split and return to coalesce, while the
\pm
non-uniqueness is depicted on the right in the figure, where geodesics split and stay apart, all the way to
\infty
.

The
L/R
non-uniqueness is a feature of continuous space. Only the
\pm
non-uniqueness appears in the discrete corner growth model with exponential weights, while both
L/R
and
\pm
non-uniqueness are present in semi-discrete BLPP
[
134
,
135
]
.

To capture
L/R
non-uniqueness, we introduce the following random set of initial points. For
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, let
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
be the set of points
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that the
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
p
is not unique. In notational terms,

\displaystyle\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}

\displaystyle=\{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)\text{ for some }t>s\},.

(4.40)

Next, let

\operatorname{NU}=\textstyle\bigcup_{\xi\hskip 0.9pt\in\hskip 0.9pt\mathbb{R},\,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\hskip 0.9pt\in\hskip 0.9pt\{-,+\}}\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}.

(4.41)

Figure 4.4
:
Given the definition of
\operatorname{NU}
and the fact that the geodesics coalesce, this figure shows two possible configurations for points
(x,s)\in\operatorname{NU}
. However, due to a recent result of Bhatia
[
26
]
and Dauvergne
[
45
]
(recorded as Theorem
4.5.1
below), the configuration depicted on the right does not appear in the DL. That, is the splitting of geodesics for points in
\operatorname{NU}
must occur immediately from the initial point.

Figure
4.4
illustrates the set
\operatorname{NU}
. Theorem
4.5.2
(ii)
establishes that, with probability one, for each
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, the restriction of
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
to each time level
s
is countably infinite. By Theorem
4.6.1
(i)
, on a single event of probability one, for each direction
\xi
and sign
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, all
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesics coalesce. Therefore, from each
p\in\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
, two
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesics separate but eventually come back together. This coalescence result was first proven in the author’s joint work with Busani and Seppäläinen
[
35
]
. Afterward, Bhatia
[
26
]
gave greater clarity to the set
\operatorname{NU}
. In particular, for a point
(x,s)\in\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
, the splitting of geodesics must occur immediately from the initial point. This follows from the coalescence we proved in
[
35
]
and the following theorem, proved first Bhatia
[
26
]
and also independently by Dauvergne
[
45
]
.

Theorem 4.5.1

(
[
26
]
, Theorem 1 and
[
45
]
, Lemma 3.3)
.

With probability one, there is no point
(x,s;y,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\textstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\uparrow$}}}}^{4}
and pairs of distinct geodesics
g_{1},g_{2}
from
(x,s)
to
(y,t)
satisfying, for some
\varepsilon>0
,
g_{1}(u)=g_{2}(u)
for all
u\in(s,s+\varepsilon)\cup(t-\varepsilon,t)
. In words, geodesics cannot move together, then form bubbles, then move together again. See Figure
4.4
.

Since
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi-}\cup\operatorname{NU}^{\xi+}
captures only the
L/R
distinction and not the
\pm
distinction, it does
not
in general contain all the initial points from which the
\xi
-directed semi-infinite geodesic is not unique. However, when the
\xi\pm
distinction is absent, Theorem
4.5.7
(i)
implies that
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi}=\operatorname{NU}^{\xi\pm}
is exactly the set of points
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that the semi-infinite geodesic from
p
in direction
\xi
is not unique. This happens under two scenarios: when
\xi\notin\Xi
, and when we restrict attention to the
\xi
-dependent event of full probability on which
g_{p}^{\xi-,S}=g_{p}^{\xi+,S}
for all
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
S\in\{L,R\}
.

The failure to capture the
\pm
non-uniqueness is also evident from the size of
\operatorname{NU}
. Whenever
\xi\in\Xi
, there are at least two semi-infinite geodesics with direction
\xi
from
every
initial point. But along a fixed time level
\operatorname{NU}
is countable, and thereby a strict subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
(Theorem
4.5.2
(ii)
below).

Recall that
\mathcal{H}_{s}=\{(x,s):x\in\mathbb{R}\}
is the set of space-time points at time level
s
. Theorem
4.4.5
(iii)
states that on a single event of full probability,
\Xi\subseteq\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Q}
, so for
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
, we can drop the
\pm
distinction and write
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi}=\operatorname{NU}^{\xi-}=\operatorname{NU}^{\xi+}
.

Theorem 4.5.2
.

On a single event of probability one, the set
\operatorname{NU}
satisfies

\operatorname{NU}=\textstyle\bigcup_{\xi\in\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{NU}^{\xi}.

(4.42)

In particular, the following hold.

(i)

For each
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}(p\in\operatorname{NU})=0
, and the full-probability event of the theorem can be chosen so that
\operatorname{NU}
contains no points of
\mathbb{Q}^{2}
.

(ii)

On a single event of full probability, simultaneously for every
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, the set
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}\cap\,\mathcal{H}_{s}
is countably infinite and unbounded in both directions. Specifically, for each
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exist sequences
x_{n}\to-\infty
and
y_{n}\to+\infty
such that
(x_{n},s),(y_{n},s)\in\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
. By (
4.42
),
\operatorname{NU}\cap\,\mathcal{H}_{s}
is also countably infinite.

Remark 4.5.3
.

The set
\mathbb{Q}
can be replaced by any countable dense subset of
\mathbb{R}
, by adjusting the full-probability event. In all applications in this chapter, we use the set
\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 4.5.4
.

As Item
(ii)
states, the set
\operatorname{NU}
is countably infinite along each horizontal line. Hence, the set
\operatorname{NU}
is uncountable. Bhatia
[
26
]
recently proved that the Hausdorff dimension of
\operatorname{NU}
is
\frac{4}{3}
.

The next theorem states properties of Busemann geodesics that involve the
L/R
and
\pm
distinctions.

Theorem 4.5.5
.

The following hold on a single event of full probability.

(i)

For
s<t
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}-,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}+,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}-,S}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}+,S}(t).

(ii)

Let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, let
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be a compact set, and let
T>\max K
. Then, there exists a random
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\xi,T,K)>0
such that, whenever
\xi-\varepsilon<\alpha<\xi<\beta<\xi+\varepsilon
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,
S\in\{L,R\}
, and
x,s\in K
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,S}(t)\qquad\text{and}\qquad g_{(x,s)}^{\beta{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,S}(t)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[s,T].

(iii)

For each
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
t>s
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,

\lim_{\xi\to\pm\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=\pm\infty.

(iv)

For all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,
s<t
and
x<y
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)\leq g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)
. More generally, if
x<y
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
g_{1}
is a
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
(x,s)
and
g_{2}
is a
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
(y,s)
such that
g_{1}(t)=g_{2}(t)
for some
t>s
, then
g_{1}(u)=g_{2}(u)
for all
u>t
. In other words, if
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
intersect, they coalesce at their first point of intersection.

(v)

For all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,
S\in\{L,R\}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and
s<t
,

\lim_{w\nearrow x}g_{(w,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t),\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{y\searrow x}g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t),

(4.43)

and if
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)=:g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)
, then for
S\in\{L,R\}
,

\lim_{(w,u)\rightarrow(x,s)}g_{(w,u)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t).

(4.44)

Furthermore,

\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=\pm\infty.

(4.45)

Remark 4.5.6
.

In general, Theorem
4.5.5
(i)
cannot be extended to mix
L
with
R
. Pick a point
(x,s)\in\operatorname{NU}
, where
\operatorname{NU}
is defined as in (
4.41
). Then, on the full-probability event of Theorem
4.5.2
, there exists a rational direction
\xi
and
t>s
such that

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,L}(t)<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,R}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t).

By Theorem
4.5.5
(ii)
, we may choose
\xi_{1}<\xi<\xi_{2}
sufficiently close to
\xi
such that

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}-,L}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{2}+,L}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}-,R}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi_{1}+,R}(t).

Item
(iv)
is an extension of Item 2 of Theorem 3.4 in
[
126
]
to all directions and all pairs of initial points on the same horizontal level.
It is not true that for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
s<t
, and
x<y
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t)\leq g_{(y,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)
. This is discussed later in Remark
4.6.4
.

The next theorem controls all semi-infinite geodesics with Busemann geodesics.

Theorem 4.5.7
.

The following hold on a single event of probability one. Let

(x_{r},t_{r})_{r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}
be any net such that
t_{r}\to\infty
and
x_{r}/t_{r}\to\xi
.

(i)

Let
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. For each
r
large enough so that
t_{r}>s
, let
g_{r}:[s,t_{r}]\to\mathbb{R}
be a geodesic from
(x,s)
to
(x_{r},t_{r})
. Then, for each
t\geq s
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(t)\leq\liminf_{r\to\infty}g_{r}(t)\leq\limsup_{r\to\infty}g_{r}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}(t).

(4.46)

In particular,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}
is the leftmost and
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi+,R}
the rightmost among
all
semi-infinite geodesics from
(x,s)
in direction
\xi
.

(ii)

Let
K\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
be compact. Suppose that there is a level
t
after which all semi-infinite geodesics from
(x,s)\in K
in direction
\xi
have coalesced. For
u\geq t
, let
g(u)
be this geodesic. Then, given
T>t
, there exists
R\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that for
r\geq R
and all
(x,s)\in K
, if
g_{r}:[s,t_{r}]\to\mathbb{R}
is a geodesic from
(x,s)
to
(x_{r},t_{r})
, then

g_{r}(u)=g(u)\qquad\text{for all }u\in[t,T].

In particular, suppose there is a unique semi-infinite geodesic from
(x,s)
in direction
\xi
, denoted by
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi}
. Then given
T>s
, for sufficiently large
r
, we have

g_{r}(u)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi}(u)\qquad\text{for all }u\in[s,T].

Remark 4.5.8
.

Theorem
4.6.1
(i)
below states that the assumed coalescence in Item
(ii)
occurs whenever
\xi\notin\Xi
. The second statement of Item
(ii)
is in Corollary 3.1 in
[
126
]
. We provide a different proof that uses the regularity of the Busemann process.

4.5.1
Proofs

In this section, we prove Theorems
4.5.2
,
4.5.5
, and
4.5.7
. In each of these, the full-probability event is
\Omega_{2}
(
4.28
).
We start by proving parts of Theorem
4.5.5
, then go to the proof of Theorem
4.5.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.5.5
, Items
(i)
–
(iii)
.

Item
(i)
(monotonicity of geodesics in the direction parameter)
was already proven as Equation (
4.25
). In fact, this item holds on
\Omega_{1}
.

Item
(ii)
(geodesics agree locally for close directions):
This follows a similar proof as the proof of Theorem
4.4.1
(v)
. Let
K
be a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}
, and let
T
be an integer greater than
\max K
. Set

A=\inf\{g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi-1)-,L}(T):x,s\in K\},\qquad\text{and}\qquad B=\sup\{g_{(x,s)}^{(\xi+1)+,R}(T):x,s\in K\}.

By Lemma
4.4.13
and Item
(i)
,
-\infty<A<B<\infty
. Then, for all
0<\varepsilon<1
sufficiently small, all
\xi-\varepsilon<\alpha<\xi
, and all
x,s\in K
, the functions
z\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,T)+W_{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(z,T;0,T)
and
z\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x,s;z,t)+W_{\xi-}(z,T;0,T)
agree on the set
[A,B]
, which contains all maximizers. Hence, for such
\alpha
and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(T)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,S}(T)
. Since
g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}:[s,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
and
g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}:[s,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
define semi-infinite geodesics that are, respectively, the leftmost and rightmost geodesics between any of their points (Theorem
4.4.9
(iii)
-
(iv)
), it must also hold that for
S\in\{L,R\}
and
t\in[t,T]
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,S}(t)
.
Otherwise, taking
S=L
without loss of generality, there would exist two distinct leftmost geodesics from
(x,s)
to
(g_{(x,s)}^{\xi-,L}(T),T)
, a contradiction. The proof for the
\xi+
geodesics where
\beta
is sufficiently close to
\xi
from the right is analogous.

Item
(iii)
(limit of geodesics as direction goes to
\pm\infty)
:
This holds on
\Omega_{2}
by definition (
4.28
).

We postpone the proof of Items
(iv)
and
(v)
until after the following proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.5.2
(Description of the set
\operatorname{NU}
).

By Theorem
4.4.5
(ii)
, on the event
\Omega_{2}
,
\alpha\notin\Xi
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
, so we omit the
\pm
distinction in this case.
We first prove (
4.42
).
If
(x,s)\in\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}
then

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)
for some
t>s
. By Theorem
4.5.5
(ii)
, there exists a rational direction
\alpha
(greater than
\xi
if
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}=+
and less than
\xi
if
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}=-
) such that

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha,L}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)<g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)=g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha,R}(t).

Hence,
(x,s)\in\operatorname{NU}^{\alpha}
.

Item
(i)
:
By
Theorem
4.3.1
(iii)
, for fixed direction
\xi
and fixed initial point
p
, there is a unique semi-infinite geodesic from
p
in direction
\xi
, implying
(x,s)\notin\operatorname{NU}^{\xi}
. The result now follows directly from (
4.42
) and a union bound. In particular, by definition of the event
\Omega_{1}\supset\Omega_{2}
(
4.12
), for each
(q,r)\in\mathbb{Q}^{2}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}
,
(q,r)\notin\operatorname{NU}^{\xi}
. Then, by (
4.42
), on the event
\Omega_{2}
,
\operatorname{NU}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{Q}^{2}
.

We postpone the proof of Item
(ii)
until the end of this subsection.
∎

Remaining proofs of Theorem
4.5.5
.

Item
(iv)
(Spatial monotonicity of geodesics):
We first prove a weaker result. Namely, for
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
x<y
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, and
S\in\{L,R\}
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)\leq g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)\qquad\text{for all }t\geq s.

(4.47)

By continuity of geodesics, it suffices to assume that
z:=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)=g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)
for some
t>s
, and then show that
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(u)=g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(u)
for all
u>t
.
By Theorem
4.4.9
(iii)
, if
z:=g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)=g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)
, then for
u>t
, both
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(u)
and
g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(u)
are the leftmost maximizer of
\mathcal{L}(z,t;w,u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(w,u;0,u)
over
w\in\mathbb{R}
, so they are equal.

Now, to prove the stated result, we follow a similar argument as Item 2 of Theorem 3.4 in
[
126
]
, adapted to give a global result across all direction, signs, and pairs of points along the same horizontal line. Let
g_{1}
be a
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
(x,s)
and let
g_{2}
be a
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
(y,s)
, and assume that
g_{1}(t)=g_{2}(t)
for some
t>s
. By continuity of geodesics, we may take
t
to be the minimal such time. Choose
r\in(s,t)\cap\mathbb{Q}
and then choose
q\in(g_{1}(r),g_{2}(r))\cap\mathbb{Q}
. See Figure
4.5
. By Theorem
4.5.2
(i)
, on the event
\Omega_{2}
, there is a unique
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
Busemann geodesic from
(q,r)
, which we shall call
g=g_{(q,r)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}=g_{(q,r)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}
.
For
u\geq r
,

g_{1}(u)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(u)\leq g(u)\leq g_{(y,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(u)\leq g_{2}(u).

(4.48)

The two middle inequalities come from (
4.47
). The two outer inequalities come from the definition of
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L/R}(u)
as the left and rightmost maximizers.

By assumption and (
4.48
),
z:=g_{1}(t)=g(t)=g_{2}(t)
. By Theorem
4.4.9
(ii)
(ii)(c)
, for
u>t
,
g_{1}(u),g_{2}(u)
, and
g(u)
are all maximizers of
\mathcal{L}(z,t;w,u)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(w,u;0,u)
over
w\in\mathbb{R}
. However, since there is a unique
\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}
geodesic from
(q,r)
, there can be only one such maximizer, so the inequalities in (
4.48
) are equalities for
u\geq t
.

Figure 4.5
:
Choosing a point
(q,r)\in\mathbb{Q}^{2}
whose
\xi\tiny{\boxempty}
geodesic is unique

Item
(v)
(limits of geodesics in the spatial parameter):
We start by proving (
4.43
). We prove the statement for the limits as
w\nearrow x
, and the limits as
w\searrow x
follow analogously. By Item
(iv)
,
z:=\lim_{w\nearrow x}g_{(w,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)
exists and is less than or equal to
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)
. Further, by the same monotonicity, for all
w\in[x-1,x]
, all maximizers of
\mathcal{L}(w,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
over
y\in\mathbb{R}
lie in the common compact set
[g_{(x-1,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t),g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t)]
. By continuity of the directed landscape (Lemma
B.1.2
), as
w\nearrow x
, the function
y\mapsto\mathcal{L}(w,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
converges uniformly on compact sets to the function
y\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
. Hence, Lemma
A.1.3
implies that
z
is a maximizer of
\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
over
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
z\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)
, and
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)
is the leftmost such maximizer, equality holds.

The proof of (
4.44
) is similar: in this case, Lemma
4.4.13
implies that for all
(w,u)
sufficiently close to
(x,s)
, the maximizers of
y\mapsto\mathcal{L}(w,u;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
lie in a common compact set. Then, by Lemma
A.1.3
, every subsequential limit of
g_{(w,u)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)
as
(w,u)\to(x,s)
is a maximizer of
y\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x,s;y,t)+W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(y,t;0,t)
. By assumption, there is only one such maximizer, so the desired convergence holds.

Lastly, to show (
4.45
), we recall that the Busemann process evolves as the KPZ fixed point (Theorem
4.4.1
(iv)
). The Busemann functions are continuous and satisfy the asymptotics prescribed in Lemma
4.4.12
(iv)
. Therefore, for each
t,\xi
, and

\textstyle\boxempty

, there exists constants
a,b>0
so that
|W_{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(x,t;0,t)|\leq a+b|x|
. Lemma
3.5.3
(iii)
applied to the temporally reflected version of
\mathcal{L}
states that for sufficiently large
|x|
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},S}(t)\in(x-|x|^{2/3},x+|x|^{2/3})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.5.7
.

We remind the reader that this theorem controls arbitrary

geodesics via the Busemann geodesics.

Item
(i)
:
Let
\alpha<\xi<\beta
. By directedness of Busemann geodesics (Theorem
4.4.9
(iii)
) and the assumption
x_{r}/r_{r}\to\xi
, for all sufficiently large
r
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha-,L}(t_{r})<x_{r}<g_{(x,s)}^{\beta+,R}(t_{r}).

Since
g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha-,L}
is the leftmost geodesic between any of its points and
g_{(x,s)}^{\beta+,R}
is the rightmost (Theorem
4.4.9
(iv)
), it follows that for
u\in[s,t_{r}]
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha-,L}(u)\leq g_{r}(u)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\beta+,R}(u).

(4.49)

Hence, for all
t\geq s
,

g_{(x,s)}^{\alpha-,L}(t)\leq\liminf_{r\to\infty}g_{r}(t)\leq\limsup_{r\to\infty}g_{r}(t)\leq g_{(x,s)}^{\beta+,R}(t).

By Theorem
4.5.5
(ii)
, taking limits as
\alpha\nearrow\xi
and
\beta\searrow\xi
completes the proof.

Item
(ii)
:
Assume that all geodesics in direction
\xi
, starting from a point in the compact set
K
, have coalesced by time
t
, and for
u\geq t
, let
g(u)
be the spatial location of this common geodesic. By Item
(i)
, for all
p\in K
and
u\geq t
,

g(u)=g_{p}^{\xi-,L}(u)=g_{p}^{\xi+,R}(u).

Let
T>t
be arbitrary. By Theorem
4.5.5
(ii)
, we may choose
\alpha<\xi<\beta
such that, for all
p\in K
and
u\in[t,T]
,

g_{(g(t),t)}^{\alpha-,L}(u)=g_{p}^{\alpha-,L}(u)=g(u)=g_{p}^{\beta+,R}(u)=g_{(g(t),t)}^{\beta+,R}(u).

(4.50)

The outer equalities hold because the geodesics pass through
(g(t),t)
. With this choice of
\alpha,\beta
, by the directedness of Theorem
4.4.9
(iii)
and since
x_{r}/t_{r}\to\xi
, we may choose
r
large enough so that
t_{r}\geq T
and

g_{(g(t),t)}^{\alpha-,L}(t_{r})<x_{r}<g_{(g(t),t)}^{\beta+,R}(t_{r}).

Then, as in the proof of Item
(i)
, for all
u\in[t,t_{r}]
,

g_{(g(t),t)}^{\alpha-,L}(u)\leq g_{r}(u)\leq g_{(g(t),t)}^{\beta+,R}(u).

Combining this with (
4.50
) completes the proof.
∎

It remains to prove Theorem
4.5.2
(ii)
. We first prove a lemma.

Lemma 4.5.9
.

Let
\omega\in\Omega_{2}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
,
\mathbb{Q}\ni s<t\in\mathbb{R}
, and assume that there is a nonempty interval
I=(a,b)\subseteq\mathbb{R}
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{Q}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)\notin I

(
By Theorem
4.5.2
(i)
, we may ignore the
L/R
distinction when
(x,s)\in\mathbb{Q}^{2}
)
. Then, there exists
\hat{x}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

g_{(\hat{x},s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},L}(t)\leq a<b\leq g_{(\hat{x},s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}},R}(t).

(4.51)

Proof.

Choose some
y\in(a,b)
, and let

\hat{x}=\sup\{x\in\mathbb{Q}:g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)<y\}.

By Equation (
4.45
) of Theorem
4.5.5
(v)
,
\hat{x}\in\mathbb{R}
. By the monotonicity of Theorem
4.5.5
(iv)
, for all
\mathbb{Q}\ni x<\hat{x}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)<y
, while for all
\mathbb{Q}\ni x>\hat{x}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)\geq y
. By assumption of the lemma,
this further implies that for
\mathbb{Q}\ni x<\hat{x}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)\leq a
while for
\mathbb{Q}\ni x>\hat{x}
,
g_{(x,s)}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}(t)\geq b
. By taking limits via Equation (
4.43
) of Theorem
4.5.5
(v)
, we obtain (
4.51
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.5.2
(ii)
(
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{s}
is countably infinite and unbounded).

We prove

the statement in three steps. First, we show that on
\Omega_{2}
, for all
s\in\mathbb{Q}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, the set
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}\cap\,\mathcal{H}_{s}
is infinite and unbounded in both directions. Next, we show that, on
\Omega_{2}
,
\operatorname{NU}^{\xi{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}}\cap\,\mathcal{H}_{s}
is in fact infinite and unbounded in both directions for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Lastly, we show that the set
\operatorname{NU}\cap\,\mathcal{H}_{s}
(the union over all directions and signs) is countable.

For the first step, Theorem
4.5.2
(i)
states that, on the event
\Omega_{2}
, for each
(x,s)\in\mathbb{Q}^{2}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, and
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\textstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\scalebox{0.9}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}}\in\{-,+\}
, there is a unique
